## 2026-11-28 (3) — Interpolation drag Smooth: densify, relax, fit back
- AI model: Claude
- Category: Fixed (no `.inb` format change; the smoothness is session state)
- Report (two screenshots, Smooth 0 % and 100 % on a zigzag in-between): the slider does not smooth, if anything it sharpens; should points be added?
- Cause (found by reading `relaxStroke`, `Vector.kt`): it ran Taubin smoothing on the line's few CONTROL points only. The stretches between sparse points were pulled straight (the opposite of the drawn Catmull-Rom curve between them) while every numbered anchor, including the tip anchors the automatic anchors put on each bend, was pinned. The curvature therefore moved into the pinned tips: sharper tips, straighter sides, and a result that differs from the 0 % curve as soon as the slider moves. The "no points added" rule made it impossible to round a tip at all. Yes, points have to be added.
- Fix (`relaxStroke`, `Vector.kt`, one function): (1) densify the line ALONG THE DRAWN CURVE (`VectorTessellator.curveAt`, about total length / 200 spacing, at most 60 extra points per span; the source points stay in the list, pressure and elevation are interpolated); (2) relax the dense points toward the middle of their neighbours, `round(128 * amount^2)` passes (at least 1), so the reach grows with the slider (a numerical check on a 6-segment zigzag: tips moved 16 / 33 / 49 / 66 units of 300 at 25 / 50 / 75 / 100 %, a large arc lost 3 of 300 at 100 %); a Taubin pair was tried first and rejected because its pass band is flat: after the first few passes the amount had no effect; (3) fit the result back with the Brush's curve-aware fitter (`VectorSimplify.fitIndices`, `WARP_FIT_EPS`), so points are added only where the line bends. Open lines: the two ends stay. Closed lines: relaxed as a ring and fitted as a ring (the seam point and its two neighbours are kept). Never moved: the open ends, hidden points, structural ends / aliases, junction points. Numbered anchors keep their number and are never dropped by the fit, but they DO move with the line (a rounded tip is no longer at the sharp tip); before they were pinned. Fills and lines of fewer than 3 points are left alone, as before.
- Preview and Apply use the same function (`VectorInterpolation.finish`), so what the bar shows is what is kept; one undo step as before; joints still land exactly where they were put (smoothing runs before the joints, unchanged). The bar's text now says "points follow the bends" instead of "no points added".
- Vector line rules (AI_WORKING_AGREEMENT section 4): follows them: dense points are fitted back with `VectorSimplify.fitIndices` (no second simplifier), anchors / ends / hidden points are protected from dropping, preview = Apply, one undo step, no `.inb` change. Exception stated above: tip anchors move with their rounded tip.
- Files: `Vector.kt`, `VectorUi.kt` (label), `Editor.kt` (comment), `WhatsNew.kt` (note 105), `PROJECT_MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. Protected infrastructure untouched.
- Validation: source review and a Python port of the relaxation on a test zigzag and an arc (strength per amount, no instability). NOT compiled, NOT run (no Android SDK here); the fitter call and the closed-ring path are untested.
- Follow-up on the tablet: (a) the same zigzag at 0 / 25 / 50 / 100 %: tips round progressively, nothing straightens, the picture at a tiny amount matches 0 %; (b) point count before / after (should stay small); (c) a line with numbered tips: numbers stay on the tips; (d) a closed line (seam stays put, no kink there); (e) lines with hidden points or branches; (f) Apply: the committed in-between equals the preview; Undo; (g) time per frame of the slider on a drawing with many lines. If 100 % is too weak or too strong, the constant is `128` (reach about 4 % of the line's length) in `relaxStroke`.

The partners' point lists replace the points of the current lines (id, colour, width and brush stay); ONE translation + uniform scale for the whole group puts the centre of the partners' joint bounding box on the centre of the selected lines' joint bounding box and gives it the same diagonal, so the group keeps its place and size and only the lines' shapes and mutual positions change (this is my reading of "doesn't move or scale the lines as a whole, only their positions to each other"; the rule is in `VectorShapeFlip.shapes`). Apply (`VectorShapeFlip.apply`) is one undo step through `replaceVectorCel`; the changed lines stay selected as whole lines. State: `Editor.vecShapeCel` (session only, reset on selection / frame / tool change).

Junction numbers of the borrowed points are cleared and `VectorJunctions.normalize` drops links that no longer join two lines, so a branch link on a flipped line is lost (known limit). Lines of the selection with no partner in that keyframe stay as they are and do not take part in the group placement.
- Files: `VectorUi.kt`, `VectorShapes.kt` (new), `Canvas.kt`, `Editor.kt`, `WhatsNew.kt` (note 103), `PROJECT_MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. Protected infrastructure untouched.

## 2026-11-27 (12) — Build fix: `vecExpandLinked` declared before `vecSelect`
- AI model: Claude
- Category: Fixed (compile error; no behavior change, no format change)
- Build log (GitHub Actions run, step "Build signed release APK", task `:app:compileReleaseKotlin`): `Canvas.kt:1242:56 Unresolved reference 'vecExpandLinked'.` It was the only error.
- Cause: `vecSelect` and `vecExpandLinked` (entry 11) are local functions of the `DrawingCanvas` composable. A Kotlin local function cannot be called above its declaration, and `vecExpandLinked` was declared three lines below `vecSelect`.
- Fix: in `Canvas.kt` the declaration of `vecExpandLinked` now stands directly above `vecSelect`; the bodies are unchanged (a pure move, plus a one-line comment). Protected infrastructure (workflow, Gradle, signing) untouched: the failure was in source code only.
- Validation: read the build log and the code; the diff is the move only. Not compiled here (no Android SDK / Gradle), not run. The next CI build is the real check; entry 11's tablet follow-up is still open.
- Rule for later work: in `DrawingCanvas` (Canvas.kt) a new local `fun` must be placed above every function that calls it.

## 2026-11-27 (11) — Linked lines are selected together; the join after an erase keeps the outer end numbers
- AI model: Claude
- Category: Fixed / Changed (junction links; no format change)
- Request (after a tablet screenshot): selecting one line must select the lines linked to it (moving one displaced the linked line at the link point only); interpolation broke after erasing the line between the linked points.
- `Vector.kt` `VectorJunctions.expandSelection`: every wholly selected line brings in the whole lines on the other side of its junctions, through the whole tree (lines with only some points selected bring in nothing). `Canvas.kt` `vecSelect` (tap / lasso of the Selection tool, add and replace; not subtract) applies it through `vecExpandLinked` per cel, so the lines move as one and `follow` finds the partners already moved.
- Cause found by reading for the interpolation: `joinLinkedEnds` used `mid`, which keeps both ends' numbers on the joint as an end identity and lets AutoAnchors.refresh hand the second one to the new OUTER ends. The outer ends were renumbered, so the joined line no longer shared its numbers with the lines of the other keyframe (`pairAuto` / `pairSelected` pair by shared numbers). Now the joint is a plain in-between point with ONE number (an in-between number of either side first, else the first line's end number); the other side's number is dropped and nothing is passed on to the outer ends.
- Known limit: lines are paired one to one. When the other keyframe still has the branched lines (the part between the junctions not erased there), the joined line pairs with only one of them and the other one fades; erase the same part in both keyframes. Not compiled, not run.
- Follow-up on the tablet: (a) tap one line of a branch: all linked lines are selected and move rigidly; (b) erase the part between two branches in two keyframes: the inbetween between them does not break and the outer end numbers did not change; (c) tap a single point (Points mode): only the linked points follow.

## 2026-11-27 (10) — Junctions: move together, tap selects the whole junction, branches rejoin after an erase / delete
- AI model: Claude
- Category: Added (builds on entry 9; no format change)
- Request: go ahead with the next steps of the branch links.
- `Vector.kt` `VectorJunctions.follow(orig, edited)`: after an edit that kept the lines and points, a moved junction point drags its partners that sat on the same spot before (within 0.8 px) and did not move themselves; the rest of the partner's line stays. `Canvas.kt` `vecApplyAll` runs it per cel on every live edit (point drag, transform, warp), so the branch stays attached while it is moved. Duplicated pairs of lines with the same numbers at other spots are left alone.
- `Canvas.kt` `vecEditDown` / `vecJunctionKeys`: tapping a point that has a junction selects its partners too (a lasso takes them anyway: they sit on the same spot).
- `VectorMerge.joinLinkedEnds(strokes, sharp)`: two linked points that are both ends of open lines (typically after the part of a line between two branches was erased or deleted, which cuts exactly on the shared point) are joined like an end-to-end merge (`join` / `mid`: both ends' numbers kept, AutoAnchors.refresh relocates them), the link is gone, repeated until none is left. A junction with three or more members or an inside point stays. Called first thing in `Canvas.vecRefreshAnchors` (eraser, pen merge) and `AutoAnchors.refreshFor` (selection Delete / Merge), and in the Delete button when auto anchors are off; the joined lines count as changed for the anchor refresh.
- Not done yet: interpolation reading a junction as one in-between anchor; points moved by tools that do not go through `vecApplyAll` (numeric / key nudges, alignment) do not drag partners; the numbers at the new joint after the join follow the existing join rules (the user may still see renumbering there, to be tuned next). Not compiled, not run.

## 2026-11-27 (9) — Junction links: branches between lines (storage + Merge creates them + shown on the canvas)
- AI model: Claude
- Category: Added (data model, `.inb` metadata without a version bump)
- Request: merging one line onto another must make them one connected line with branches (a line sprouting from any point along another), the target line staying whole; moving together and the erase step come later.
- Finding: a stroke is one path with two ends, so a branch cannot be one stroke. Splitting the target (tried and backed out) cut it; copying the end's number onto it (also backed out) left two unconnected lines.
- `Vector.kt`: `VectorPoint.junction: Int` (0 = none): points of different lines with the same number are one junction. Stored as sparse Ink metadata `JUNCTION_OFFSET (4_000_000) + point index, number` (same scheme as the alias / endpoint / auto markers; older builds skip it as out of range; read first in `toVectorStroke`). New `VectorJunctions`: `groups`, `partners`, `newId` (< 2^23, exact as float), `normalize` (drops numbers that no longer link two different lines). `VectorMerge.attach` (pen Merge) and `mergePoints` (Selection Merge) give the end and the spot it lands on on the other line the same junction number (reusing the target point's number when it already has one); the target line is not cut and gets no copied anchor. Junction points are protected from thinning like anchors (`fixed` / `protect` in the simplifiers). `AutoAnchors.refreshFor` and `Canvas.vecRefreshAnchors` call `normalize`. `Canvas.kt`: a cyan ring is drawn around a point that has a junction.
- Not done yet (by design, next steps): moving one point of a junction moves its partners; selecting one selects the others; erasing the part between two junctions joins the rest into one line keeping the numbers; interpolation reading the junction as one in-between anchor. Links of points that are moved apart stay recorded until a refresh finds the partner gone.
- Validation: source review and a brace / parenthesis count only. Not compiled, not run on a device. Check the next GitHub build log first.
- Follow-up on the tablet: (a) pen Merge / Selection Merge an end onto a line: both points show a cyan ring and nothing is cut; (b) draw, save, reopen the project: the rings are still there; (c) Undo / Redo keep them; (d) erase the line it sprouts from: the ring on the other point disappears at the next refresh.

## 2026-11-27 (8) — Merge onto the body of a line (on the drawn curve), Merge button with Points off, ghost numbers re-inherited after an erase / delete
- AI model: Claude
- Category: Fixed / Added (vector merge and anchors; no `.inb` format change)
- Request: (1) merging the sole line onto the boot line did not work; no line merges onto another anywhere but at the ends; (2) after erasing a line, the merged line should inherit the anchors of the erased line by the dynamic anchor rules, with new ones where needed.
- (1) Cause found by reading: every body test (`VectorMerge.attach` T junction, `snapTarget`, `mergePoints` step 2) measured the distance to the control POLYGON of the other line. Lines are drawn as smooth curves through as few points as their shape needs (vector line rules), so such a line bows far from its polygon: a line touching another one on screen was often farther than the reach from its polygon, and only the ends (which are real points) could ever be hit. `Vector.kt`: new `VectorMerge.onBody` / `BodyHit` measure on the drawn curve (`VectorTessellator.curveAt`, 16 samples per segment plus a short refinement), return the place on the curve (and the control point when the touch is on one, so nothing is inserted there), and never offer the ends of an open line. The three call sites use it; the vertex is inserted on the curve exactly where the end lands, so both lines share it, as before. Crossings between two bodies are unchanged (still found on the control polygons).
- (1) `VectorUi.kt`: the Merge button of the Selection bar was only shown with Points on. It is now shown with Points off too (selected whole lines offer their two ends, within the Merge radius). The toast text differs per mode.
- (2) Cause found by reading: after a delete / erase only lines whose end moved are refreshed, and a line follows a ghost only when it already carries the ghost's end numbers. The erased line carried them, the merged line had fresh numbers, so nothing ever handed the freed ghost numbers on. `AutoAnchors.reclaimFreed(before, after, refs, sens)`: a ghost line whose end numbers were carried by lines of `before` and by no line of `after` is free; the nearest line of `after` that follows no ghost line (within 0.35 x the removed line's size + 10 px, mean distance of its points to the removed line) inherits it through `AutoAnchors.inherit` (ends, then in-between numbers by position and bend, new numbers for apexes without a partner). Called at the end of `AutoAnchors.refreshFor` (selection Merge / Delete) and `Canvas.kt` `vecRefreshAnchors` (pen merge, eraser). Does nothing when the edit freed no ghost line.
- Vector line rules (AI_WORKING_AGREEMENT section 4): the merge inserts one vertex on the curve where the end lands and moves nothing else; anchors and structural ends are not touched except by the usual anchor layout; one undo step as before; `.inb` unchanged.
- Follow-up (same day, after a tablet screenshot): with Points off the Merge radius / Sharp joins sliders were not shown at all, so the reach stayed at the 14 px default and could not be widened for whole-line Merge. `VectorUi.kt`: the slider box is now shown with the Selection tool whether Points is on or off (not on the interpolation preview). In the screenshot one end of the moved line snapped and the other, about 30 screen px from the other line, stayed out of reach; not confirmed to be the only cause.
- Follow-up 2 (same day): an attempt to make an end drawn onto the body of a line one stroke by SPLITTING the target line was backed out at once: it cut the target line (a piece was left hanging apart). The T junction is back to the shared vertex; no line is ever cut by Merge. What "one merged line" should mean for an end on a body is still to be decided with the user.
- Follow-up 3 (same day): the duplicate anchor number on the target line (`pinJunctions`) is REMOVED again. It left two separate lines with a copied number and no real merge. Replaced by junction links (see the next entry).
- Files: `Vector.kt`, `VectorUi.kt`, `AutoAnchors.kt`, `Canvas.kt`, `WhatsNew.kt` (note 102), `PROJECT_MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. Protected files untouched.
- Validation: source review and a diff review only. No Kotlin compiler in the sandbox, so NOT compiled; not run on a device. Check the next GitHub build log first.
- Follow-up on the tablet: (a) pen Merge and the Selection Merge with a sole end a few px from the boot's curve (also where the boot has few points): the end snaps on, the boot gets a vertex, the shimmer goes; (b) Merge button with Points off; (c) with the ghost on, trace a line, merge, erase the traced line: the merged line takes the ghost numbers, apexes paired sensibly, Undo restores; (d) no change when erasing a line that follows no ghost. Open: the nearest-line rule can pick a wrong line when several unclaimed lines lie near the erased one; not measured.

## 2026-11-27 (7) — Live points, New frame after the playhead, Select ghost line, timeline pinch pan, Pin Warp fitting, pinch-reset disengage, vector behavior rule
- AI model: Claude
- Category: Fixed / Added (no `.inb` format change)
- Request: (1) vector points do not update in real time; (2) New frame should create a frame after the playback line and put us on it; (3) a Select ghost line button on the bar; (4) timeline pinch zoom should also pan to where the pinch happens; (5) Pin Warp on vector lines should place points intelligently and put curves between them, and this goes into the md file as a behavior rule for future vector features; (6) allow disengaging the canvas pinch-to-reset by spreading the fingers apart.
- (1) `Canvas.kt`. Cause found by reading: the point overlay (`vecShowStrokes`) is rebuilt on `tick`, but the live apply of a Transform / Warp / Puppet session ran later (`vecXfRefresh`, from a `LaunchedEffect`) without bumping `tick`, and a drag edit skipped by the 14 ms throttle (`vecThrottled`) waited for the next pen event or the release. So the overlay showed the strokes of the previous event and stopped short when the pen paused. `vecApplyAll` (the funnel of every live edit) now bumps `tick` after applying, and `vecThrottled` applies the skipped edit 16 ms later with a main-thread `Handler` (cleared on release). Not reproduced on a device.
- (2) `Editor.kt` `addFrame`: the new empty frame goes right after the playhead frame. Before, it went after the whole cel that contains the playhead, so inside a hold (for example 4b x9) it landed at the end of the hold. Now, when the playhead is inside a hold and not on its last frame, the cel is split: the part up to the playhead keeps its drawing, then the new frame (duration 1), then the rest of the hold as a LINKED clone (`cloneLinked`, same drawing, `vecCfg` kept). Everything after shifts one frame, nothing else changes. The playhead moves onto the new frame. Interpretation: I read "after where the playback line is" as the frame after the playhead, not after its hold; tell me if you meant something else.
- (3) `AutoAnchors.kt` `LineGhost.pick`: pins the ghost line nearest to a document point (traced ones included, within 28 screen px). `Editor.kt` `vecGhostPick`; `VectorUi.kt`: toggle button (`TouchApp`) after Unclaim in the ghost controls; `Canvas.kt`: while it is on, the next pen-down on a vector layer picks instead of drawing / selecting, then turns itself off (toast "No ghost line there" when nothing is close). Pen only, like the other vector tools.
- (4) `Panels.kt`: the timeline pinch is measured from the left edge of the frame track (it was measured from the panel's left edge, which includes the layer names, so the anchor was off by their width), uses the whole-pixel cell widths before and after (`pxForScale`), and applies the wanted scroll after the new width has been laid out (`pendingScroll`, same two-frame wait the Fit button uses), because `dispatchRawDelta` was clamped to the OLD content width while zooming in. Two-finger pan is part of the same target.
- (5) `VecXform.kt` `vecDeform`: whole lines are now fitted back with the Brush's curve-aware fitter (`VectorSimplify.fitIndices`, `WARP_FIT_EPS`) for closed lines as well (ring fitted as an open run from its first point; the seam point and its two neighbours are kept). `Canvas.kt` `vecXfRefresh`: the live preview uses the same thinning as Apply, so the points and curves you see while pinning are what Apply keeps. Anchors, structural ends and hidden points stay protected. Selections of single points (Points mode) are unchanged on purpose: they keep the point count and the selection keys.
- (5) `AI_WORKING_AGREEMENT.md` section 4: new "Vector line behavior" rules for every present and future vector feature (few points, smooth curves, add points where the shape bends then fit with the Brush fitter, protected points, live = applied, Points mode keeps point count, closed lines, one undo step).
- (6) `Canvas.kt`: the pinch reset stays armed once the two fingers come within `RESET_PINCH_DP`, as before; spreading them beyond 1.6 times that distance disarms it (and hides the cue), so lifting then leaves the view alone. Bringing them close again re-arms it.
- `WhatsNew.kt` note 101, `PROJECT_MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. Protected files untouched.
- Validation: source review of the diff and a brace-balance check only. Not compiled, not run on a device.
- Follow-up: on the tablet check (1) points follow the pen during point drag, Transform, Warp and Puppet pins, also when the pen pauses; (2) New frame in the middle of a hold and at the last frame of a hold, Undo; with a vector layer too; (3) Select ghost line picks a traced and an untraced line; (4) pinch the timeline with the fingers at the left, middle and right of the frames: the frame under the fingers stays put; (5) Puppet Warp a long open line and a closed shape: few points, smooth, anchors kept, Apply equals the preview; (6) pinch together then apart: no reset; together and lift: reset. Not measured: the time the live thinning takes per frame on a very long line.

## 2026-11-27 (5) — Selection merge: the longest line's end anchors become the new ends
- AI model: Claude
- Category: Fixed (vector anchors on merge; no `.inb` format change)
- Request: a merge of two points was still not reflected on the ghost line; suspected conflict over which anchor becomes the new end. Rule requested: the largest line's end anchors go to the ends of the merged line.
- Found by reading: line length played no part. `AutoAnchors.selfPlan` chose the two end numbers of a merged line by cost (a number shared with a neighbouring keyframe first, then the end's own number, then how close the number's old position was to the end), and `ghostFor` returned the FIRST ghost line sharing any number, so a short stub or the list order could decide. `VectorMerge.mid` listed the join's numbers in drawing order.
- Scope (your correction): ONLY the Merge button of the point selection bar. The pen merge (`attach`, `vecRefreshAnchors`) keeps its previous behaviour, which works.
- `Vector.kt`: `join(..., byLength)` is opt-in; only `mergePoints` passes `true`, so the longer line's numbers come first at its joins. `attach` keeps drawing order.
- `AutoAnchors.kt`: `piecesOf` / `Pieces` split a merged open line at its join points (points that carry end aliases until the next refresh). `refresh(..., longest = true)` is passed only by `refreshFor` (selection merge / delete); `Canvas.kt` `vecRefreshAnchors` does not. `selfPlan`: the two numbers bounding the LONGEST piece become the end numbers of the merged line (its own far end keeps its number, the number at its join moves to the new far end); the old cost search still decides an end for which the longest piece has no number. `ghostFor`: among the ghost lines whose numbers the line carries, the one matched by the longest piece wins; ties keep the first, as before.
- Not changed: `ghostPlan` (the ghost-following case) still gives the ghost's end numbers by orientation; only the choice of WHICH ghost changed.
- `WhatsNew.kt` note 99, `PROJECT_MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. Protected files untouched. `.inb` format unchanged.
- Validation: source review of the diff only. Not compiled, not run on a device; I could not reproduce your case, so it is unconfirmed that this is the cause.
- Follow-up: on the tablet, merge two traced pieces (long + short) with Merge on selected points and with the pen, with the ghost on, and check the merged line carries the long piece's numbers and the ghost line turns yellow-orange. Also try merging a piece that followed ghost line X onto a longer piece that followed ghost line Y. If it still fails, note the anchor numbers of both lines and of the ghost line before and after.

## 2026-11-27 (4) — Ghost lines wrongly shown as traced: cause narrowed, manual Unclaim button
- AI model: Claude
- Category: Fixed / Added (vector line ghost; no `.inb` format change)
- Request: inspect why ghost lines are sometimes wrongly claimed, and add a manual unclaim button for the current ghost line.
- How a claim works (read from the code): a ghost line counts as traced (`LineGhost.isClaimed`) when ANY anchor number or end alias on ANY point of ANY line of the current keyframe equals one of the ghost line's end numbers (all its numbers for a closed ghost). There is no check of position or shape; it is purely number equality. `Canvas.kt` `vecAnchorNew` uses the same test (and then refuses to number the new stroke).
- Cause found by reading: the numbers a new line is given avoid `AutoAnchors.neighbourNumbers`, which looked only at the nearest previous / next keyframe with lines. The ghost, however, can come from the frames picked in the timeline (2026-11-26 (7)), which need not be those keyframes. A line numbered fresh could therefore reuse an end number of the picked ghost frame, and that ghost line then showed yellow-orange although nothing traced it. The same collision can follow from reordering frames, files from older builds, or a frame drawn while its reference frame was empty. Not reproduced on a device.
- Candidate causes NOT changed (needs your decision, see follow-up): the claim also counts interior curve-tip numbers and hidden points of current lines, although the doc comment says only ends count; stale `endpointAliases` left on a point count as well.
- `AutoAnchors.kt`: `neighbourNumbers(layer, idx, ed)` also includes the numbers of the picked reference frames (`refreshFor` passes `ed`). `LineGhost.claimNumbers` (a ghost line's claim numbers) and `LineGhost.unclaim(ed, cut, layer)`: for the ghost line now shown, every point of the current keyframe carrying one of its claim numbers gets a new number that no line of this keyframe, of the neighbouring / picked reference keyframes or of the ghost frame uses (aliases are dropped; if numbers run out the anchor is cleared). Lines keep their anchors; one undo step through `replaceVectorCel`; the ghost line stays pinned.
- `Canvas.kt`: the four fresh-numbering / merge call sites that avoid neighbour numbers now pass `ed`. The ghost-frame call (`gTaken`) is unchanged.
- `VectorUi.kt`: unclaim button (`LinkOff` icon) in the ghost controls, next to previous / next ghost line; shows a short message when nothing was claiming the line.
- `WhatsNew.kt` note 98, `PROJECT_MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. Protected files untouched.
- Validation: source review of every call site and the diff only. Not compiled, not run on a device (no Gradle / Kotlin compiler in the sandbox).
- Follow-up: on the tablet, reproduce a wrong claim, tap Unclaim and check the line turns teal and traces again, and that Undo restores it. Decide whether `isClaimed` / the `vecAnchorNew` refusal should count only the ENDS of current lines (matches the documented rule, would stop interior-tip collisions; both places must change together). Existing wrong numbers are not repaired automatically.

## 2026-11-26 — Ghost tracing matches anchors by position and bend; anchor numbers up to 999 (format V29 when needed)
- AI model: Claude
- Category: Changed / Added (source code, format V29 only when needed)
- Request: (1) smarter in-between anchors when tracing a ghost: decide by distance from the end points which bend of the new line takes which ghost anchor, and only then add new anchors for extra bends between them; a ghost anchor with no fitting bend is dropped; (2) raise the highest anchor number to 999.
- (1) `AutoAnchors.kt`. New private `Desc` / `bendAt` / `pairCost` / `matchDescs`. `bendAt(dense, u)` = signed turn (degrees) between the chord arriving from and the chord leaving a short way (same window as the apex detection) around progress `u`. `pairCost` = half the plain difference of progress + half the difference of the distance from the NEAREST end (plain difference when the two lie on different halves), plus, when both bends are clear (>= 6 degrees), 0.5 for opposite sides (always refused) or up to 0.1 for a different sharpness. `matchDescs` = ordered alignment of least total cost; a pair is allowed up to 0.45, an unpaired apex or ghost tip costs 0.25. Equal counts are no longer paired blindly. `PlanTip` gets `turn` (the ghost's bend read in the direction the new line runs: sign flipped when the ghost is read backwards). `Plan` gets `smart` and `freshTips`; `ghostPlan` sets both for open-over-open traces only. `layout`: with `smart` the new matcher replaces `matchOrdered`; with `freshTips` apexes no ghost tip took get new numbers, ends never do (only `fresh` numbers ends), and every number of the ghost plan is kept out of the fresh pool so a dropped ghost tip cannot get a wrong partner. Closed lines (`matchCyclic`, ring plans) and `selfPlan` (refresh after merge / delete, new lines) are unchanged and still use `matchOrdered`. `refresh` uses `ghostPlan` for a line that follows a ghost, so that case gets the new matching too.
- (2) `ANCHOR_MAX` 99 -> 999 (`Vector.kt`). The sparse anchor list stored rank + 128 for hidden points and read it with `and 127`, so the old encoding cannot hold more than 127. New `encodeAnchorRank` / `decodeAnchorRank`: numbers 0..99 keep the old values (identical bytes and readable by older builds), numbers 100..999 are written as 1000 + number (hidden: 3000 + number). `Store.kt`: the value was written as ONE BYTE (clamped to 255), so larger values would have been lost on save. New format V29: the value is a variable-length number. V29 is written only when some stroke has a value above 255; it has the V28 layout (guide counts per keyframe and the Uniform flags block are then always written, `usesGuides` is true for it). Files without such anchors are written as before (V25 / V26 / V28). Reader: V1-V29. Older builds cannot open V29 files.
- `VectorInterpolation.anchorPairs` walked 1..ANCHOR_MAX with two `indexOfFirst` scans per number (999 x 2 scans per stroke pair, on the live interpolation path); it now builds a first-index map for each stroke. Same pairs, same order.
- Not changed: `AutoAnchors.MAX_AUTO_TIPS` (24 tips per line), the number pad (30 characters) and the callers that clamp to `ANCHOR_MAX`. Fresh numbers avoid the numbers of the ghost plan and the keyframe's own numbers but not those of the OTHER neighbouring keyframe (as before).

## 2026-11-24 — Freer guide joints, rotation magnet in Interpolation drag, second tap deletes a joint
- AI model: Claude
- Category: Changed (source code, no format change)
- Request: joints (Interpolation drag and Selection > Displace) should be more freeform; the line's own curve resisted the joint (screenshot of a toe tip with five joints close together). Interpolation drag needs a button to turn off the 30-degree rotation steps; the transformation should not have a button that fixes the line weight; a second tap on a joint deletes it and the bar's Delete button deletes all joints of the selected line(s).
- Cause of the resistance (code reading, not reproduced on a device): `guideOffset` gave every joint the same fixed reach (25 % of the line) whatever the pull, so a small nudge moved a long stretch and neighbouring joints blended their offsets over that stretch, which keeps the line's original shape between joints.
- Joint field (`Vector.kt`): `guideOffset(js, closed, p, total)` now takes the line length; each joint's reach is `guideReach` = 2.5 x its pull / line length, limited to 3 %..25 % of the line (a small move is local, a large one still reaches up to the old 25 %). Between two joints closer than the larger reach the displacement is still the exact smoothstep blend; farther apart each joint fades over its own reach, and between 1x and 2x the larger reach the two are cross-faded, so the field stays continuous while a joint is dragged (the old hard switch at 2 x reach could pop). The joint's own displacement at the joint is still exact. Shared by Interpolation drag (`warpStroke`) and Displace (`displaceStrokeWarped`). Saved joints are unchanged, so existing V27 / V28 files load and now look less stiff.
- Rotation magnet (`VectorUi.kt`): the Interpolation drag bar shows the existing rotation magnet button (`MagnetBtn`, `Editor.rotationSnap`) while Shift is on. `vecPlaceMove` already honoured `rotationSnap`; only the button was missing. A relative joint holds the OFFSET from where the unmoved line is at its arc position (`polylinePointAt`); `warpStroke` uses the offset as the joint's displacement and pins `base point + offset`. - Files: `Vector.kt`, `Store.kt`, `Editor.kt`, `Canvas.kt`, `VectorUi.kt`, `WhatsNew.kt` (note 80).

## 2026-11-23 — Ghost tracing across open / closed: open a ring ghost, close an open ghost
- AI model: Claude
- Category: Added / Fixed (source code)
- Report: a self-merged (closed) ghost line traced as an OPEN line, and an open ghost traced as a CLOSED line, lose the ghost's anchor numbers.
- Cause (code reading): `AutoAnchors.ghostPlan` returned null whenever the trace and the ghost differed in open / closed, so the trace got fresh numbers (open over ring) or, for an open trace that Merge later closed, `refresh` fell back to `selfPlan` after `dropLast` had removed the closing end point.
- Fix (`AutoAnchors.kt`): the mismatch now goes to two new plans. `planOpenOverRing`: reads the ring's numbers (anchors and end aliases) round the ring from the lowest number in the direction in which numbers grow; the seam is where they wrap. First number = first end, last number = last end, the rest are tips placed by progress round the ring from first to last. `planRingOverOpen`: every number of the ghost (end numbers included) becomes a tip, read in the direction in which the ghost's numbers grow, progress squeezed by `1 - 1/n` so the two end numbers do not land on one spot of the ring (`matchCyclic` then finds the turn). Both use no distance and no shape, only the numbers and the ghost's own geometry; numbers already used in the keyframe are left out. `refresh` (after a merge) reaches them through `ghostPlan` too.
- `Canvas.kt` `vecFreshAfterMerge`: the ring-after-merge path added earlier also accepts an OPEN ghost (its end numbers must be free).
- Unchanged: open-over-open and ring-over-ring plans, the drawing-order rule, `selfPlan`, merge rules.
- Files: `AutoAnchors.kt`, `Canvas.kt`, `WhatsNew.kt` (note 77).
- Validation: code reading and bracket balance only. Not compiled, not run on a device. Next: ring ghost traced open (numbers 1..n in drawing order, with and without Merge); ring ghost numbered descending along its points; open ghost traced closed (Merge self-close and a closed shape); open over open and ring over ring (must be as before); ghost with only two numbers.
- Known limits: a trace with fewer or more bends than the ghost pairs what lines up (the unmatched ghost numbers are not used, no new numbers are invented); a ring whose numbers do not grow in one direction around it falls back to the ascending reading.

## 2026-11-23 — Merge: extend one line only; ghost trace order by number
- AI model: Claude
- Category: Fixed (source code)
- Report: (1) a line merged from its first end point keeps the right numbering, but tracing it with the line as ghost gives a reversed order; (2) a line drawn touching another line (not merged), later extended with a merge at its other end, fuses with that other line.
- Cause 1 (code reading; not reproduced): `VectorMerge.attach` joins at the old line's first end as `join(o.reversed, cur)`, so the merged line's points run last end first; `AutoAnchors.refresh` keeps each number at its position, giving 6..1 along the points. The drawing-order rule `startsAtGhostStart` (always true) then gave the start of the trace the number of the ghost's first POINT (6).
- Fix 1 (`AutoAnchors.kt`): `startsAtGhostStart` is now `g.points.first().anchor <= g.points.last().anchor`: the start of a trace takes the ghost's lower end number, whatever the point order. Still no distance or shape involved; a trace lying on the ghost still goes by `orientationOverGhost`.
- Cause 2: the loop in `attach` (up to 4 rounds) re-tested BOTH ends of the joined line against every other line, including the far end of the older line the new piece had just extended; when that end touched a third line, the third line was joined too. The T-junction snap tested both ends as well.
- Fix 2 (`Vector.kt`, `VectorMerge.attach`): `firstFree` / `lastFree` track which ends of the combined line come from the piece just drawn. Only those ends may join (a join consumes the end that took it) or snap as a T junction. A drawn piece may still bridge two lines with its two ends. Not changed: crossings (they only add shared vertices, never fuse strokes) and ring closing.
- Files: `Vector.kt`, `AutoAnchors.kt`, `WhatsNew.kt` (note 75).
- Validation: code reading and bracket balance only. Not compiled, not run on a device. Next: extend a line at its first end, then trace it (forward and reverse); two touching unmerged lines, extend one at its other end with Merge on (they must stay two lines); a piece that touches two lines with its two ends (must still bridge).

Joint samples, numbered anchors, structural ends / aliases and hidden points are protected. Closed lines are not thinned (the open fitter would add an artificial seam). Applies to interpolation preview, commit, bake path and Displace (whole-line mode); Displace in Points mode never added points.

- Root cause (`VectorInterpolation.applyGuideJoints`, `Vector.kt`): each joint added a Gaussian bump (sigma 0.10 of the line length) to the line's EXISTING points only. (1) On a line with few points the straight pieces between a strongly moved point and barely moved neighbours show as sharp corners. (2) The summed bumps were normalised (`1 / influence`) only where they exceeded 1, and the guide sample was then hard-set to the joint position, so the joint point jumped relative to its neighbours (a kink or tick at every joint). (3) Overlapping joints averaged each other so no joint received its full displacement.
- Fix: (a) before warping, long pieces of the line are subdivided so that no gap exceeds 1/150 of the line length (new points carry interpolated position, pressure and elevation, no anchor); (b) the Gaussian sum is replaced by an exact, smooth offset field: at a joint the offset is exactly that joint's displacement; between two joints closer than 0.5 of the line length it blends from one offset to the other with a quintic smoothstep (zero slope at each joint, no overshoot); beyond that distance each joint's offset fades to zero over 0.25 of the line length (closed lines wrap around). The final hard pinning of the guide samples is kept and is now consistent with the field. Joints, their stored positions and the `.inb` format are unchanged.
- Behavior change to expect: the warp profile differs from the former Gaussian (flatter top at each joint, a defined reach of 0.25 of the line), so existing guided interpolations look slightly different.
- Files: `app/src/main/java/com/exemple/inbetween/Vector.kt`, `WhatsNew.kt` (note 69), `PROJECT_MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Protected files untouched: `.github/workflows/**`, Gradle wrapper/build scripts, `gradle.properties`, manifest, SDK/Java and signing configuration.
- Validation: not compiled (no Kotlin compiler available) and not run on a device. A Python port of the offset field was checked: exactly equal to each joint's displacement at the joints, maximum absolute value equal to the largest joint offset (no overshoot), no steps at 2000 samples, and continuous across the wrap of a closed line. Complete diff reviewed.
- Known follow-up: compile; compare the same guided interpolation before / after on the tablet; if the reach (0.25) or the flatter top should differ, it is the `reach` constant and the `smoother` function in `applyGuideJoints`.

## 2026-11-22 — Ghost tracing in pieces: no piled-up in-between anchors, correct direction (1 / 3 no longer swapped)
- AI model: Claude
- Category: Fixed / Vector ghost tracing
- Report (two screenshots): with the ghost on, the in-between anchors (2, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8) were all crowded together at one end of the drawn line, and anchors 1 and 3 were inverted although the line was started at the same place as the ghost's start.
- Root cause (`AutoAnchors.inherit`, case "new line is elsewhere", i.e. the drawing moved): (1) every still-free ghost anchor was mapped by RELATIVE progress (`ghostU * total`) onto the stroke just drawn. When that stroke is only a short first piece of the outline, the whole ghost (2 .. 8, 3) is squeezed into its few pixels; the later pieces cannot get those numbers back. (2) The ghost's direction was decided from the dot product of the two start-to-end vectors; for a short piece (or an outline whose ends lie close together) that vector is unrelated to the ghost's, so the ghost was read backwards and 1 / 3 swapped. A Python port of the old placement reproduces the crowding (a 128 px piece received 2, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 3 within its 128 px).
- Fix: (a) a piece shorter than 75 % of the remaining free ghost takes the free ghost anchors in order at the ghost's own arc-length spacing, starting at the arc where the previous pieces stopped (`max(last claimed anchor, length already traced from this ghost)`, new `AutoAnchors.tracedLength`, passed by `Canvas.kt`); anchors beyond the piece stay free for the next piece; its direction follows the ghost's local tangent (forward unless the tangent clearly opposes the piece, then the free stretch is read from its other end). (b) a full-length line elsewhere chooses its direction by comparing the 24-sample shapes forward and reversed (centroid and scale removed) and flips only on a clear win (cost below 70 % of the other); otherwise the old start-to-end vector decision stands. The aligned modes (ghost under the line), endpoints handling, anchor numbers and order, merge refresh and the `.inb` format are unchanged.
- Files: `app/src/main/java/com/exemple/inbetween/AutoAnchors.kt`, `Canvas.kt` (one call site), `WhatsNew.kt` (note 66), `PROJECT_MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Protected files untouched: `.github/workflows/**`, Gradle wrapper/build scripts, `gradle.properties`, manifest, SDK/Java and signing configuration.
- Validation: no Kotlin compiler was available, so the Kotlin was NOT compiled. A Python port with a foot-outline ghost taken from the screenshot: old placement piled 7 anchors in 128 px; new placement gives the first piece only anchor 1 (at its start) and a second piece (with `consumed` 128 px) 2 at 104 px, 4 at 214 px, 5 at 382 px; shape-based direction picked forward / reversed correctly for a full trace drawn either way (scaled 0.92 and moved). Complete diff reviewed. Not run in the app.
- Not verified / limits: which end of the ghost a piece belongs to is inferred (it starts where the previous pieces stopped, so pieces should be drawn in ghost order; a piece drawn out of order gets the wrong anchors); the ghost's size is assumed equal to the new drawing's (no scale estimate); `0.75` / `0.7` thresholds are first guesses. Follow-up: compile, then trace a moved ghost in 2 to 4 pieces and as one stroke, in both directions, with S Pen.

## 2026-11-22 — Map ghost curve anchors onto the actual traced contour
- AI model: GPT-6
- Category: Fixed / Vector ghost tracing and interpolation correspondence
- Root cause: ghost-trace classification checked only the currently available (unblocked) reference anchors. If previous traced pieces had already claimed anchors outside the current trace, the remaining subset could all be near the new stroke and incorrectly classify a partial trace as a complete near-aligned trace. In that mode, automatic curve features were matched by raw normalized ghost progress rather than by their projected progress on the actual new path, so stretched or compressed curves could receive misplaced intermediate anchors.
- Fix: overlap classification now checks all anchored points on the reference ghost, including blocked anchors. In both spatially aligned modes, ordered nearest-path projection supplies the progress used to match automatic curve features to the new stroke's detected extrema. The monotonic order constraint and original anchor numbers remain intact; moved-away traces retain the relative-progress fallback.
- No `.inb` format change. Files: `app/src/main/java/com/exemple/inbetween/AutoAnchors.kt`, `app/src/main/java/com/exemple/inbetween/WhatsNew.kt`, `PROJECT_MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Validation: compiled the actual `AutoAnchors.kt` with minimal model stubs and ran a focused Kotlin geometry harness for a partial trace with previously claimed anchors; verified remaining anchor order and projected positions; the same regression assertion fails against the pre-fix implementation (first inherited feature at 64.7 px instead of the correctly projected 55.7 px). Reviewed the complete source diff and ZIP integrity. No full Android Gradle build or tablet runtime test has been performed.

## 2026-11-22 — Keep curve apex tangent context during ghost-trace smoothing
- AI model: GPT-6
- Category: Fixed / Vector ghost tracing and curve anchors
- Root cause: `fitAnchored` split the traced path into independent spans at every correspondence anchor. The renderer's `smoothThrough` treats open-span endpoints specially when calculating Catmull–Rom tangents, so an interior curve apex became an artificial endpoint. Although the anchor sample itself survived, the rendered curve could lose or shift its visible extreme around that point.
- Fix: `fitAnchored` now runs one continuous curve-aware fit over the entire open stroke and supplies correspondence anchors, structural endpoints and merge endpoint aliases as protected sample indices. This keeps the natural neighbouring tangent context while ensuring protected anchor points cannot be removed or reordered.
- No `.inb` format change. `Canvas.kt` call site and brush fitting settings remain unchanged.

## 2026-11-22 — Keep structural endpoint identities separate from automatic curve tips

- AI model: GPT-6
- Category: Fixed / Vector anchors and ghost interpolation
- Root cause: `AutoAnchors.refresh` used one pool for structural endpoints and automatic curve-tip anchors. During refresh after merges, an intermediate curve-tip identity could therefore be reassigned to an outer endpoint, changing the endpoint's correspondence number. `VectorMerge.mid` could also discard one of two endpoint identities when their points collapsed into a shared join.
- Fix: `VectorPoint` now carries an explicit structural-endpoint marker, separate from the general automatic-anchor flag. `refresh` assigns endpoint identities only to the two outer endpoints and curve-tip identities only to interior targets. Neighbour-keyframe correspondence numbers are preferred when selecting endpoint identities. `VectorMerge.mid` carries both collapsed endpoint identities as temporary aliases until refresh relocates them. Legacy endpoint anchors are recognized by their first/last point positions.
- Persistence: endpoint markers and temporary endpoint aliases are encoded as additional sparse Ink anchor metadata (`ENDPOINT_FLAG_OFFSET` / `ENDPOINT_ALIAS_OFFSET`). Existing `.inb` version is unchanged; older readers ignore the out-of-range metadata entries.
- Files: `app/src/main/java/com/exemple/inbetween/Vector.kt`, `app/src/main/java/com/exemple/inbetween/AutoAnchors.kt`, `app/src/main/java/com/exemple/inbetween/WhatsNew.kt`, `PROJECT_MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Protected files untouched: `.github/workflows/**`, Gradle wrapper/build scripts, `gradle.properties`, manifest, SDK / Java and signing configuration.
- Validation: source-level review, serialization marker read/write path inspection, targeted Kotlin harness for endpoint-vs-tip identity refresh and collapsed-join endpoint aliases, complete diff and ZIP integrity checks. No Android Gradle build or tablet runtime test was available.

## 2026-11-22 — Ghost-traced strokes keep reference anchor numbers

- AI model: GPT-6
- Problem: a stroke traced from a ghost could receive fresh endpoint numbers (for example 3 and 4) instead of the ghost's reference numbers (1 and 2). `AutoAnchors.inherit` filtered out numbers already used in the current cel, then required all reference anchors to remain free before its relative-position fallback could run. A partial overlap therefore made inheritance fail. Even when a partial trace succeeded, the normal post-draw endpoint fallback could add unrelated numbers.
- Fix: relative-position inheritance now accepts any non-empty ordered set of available ghost anchors. `Canvas.kt` records a ghost-trace decision and prevents traced strokes from receiving fresh auto endpoints when inheritance is partial or the ghost's anchors are already represented in the current cel. The merge path still refreshes structural endpoints on the complete merged stroke.
- `.inb` format unchanged.

## 2026-11-20 — Auto anchors at ends and curve tips, merge-aware; line ghost with previous / next toggle

- AI model: Claude
- Request: anchors created automatically while drawing, at the ends and the tips of curves in between, dynamic when a line is built by merging smaller ones; a "line order onion skin" where the line to draw shimmers so the new line matches the anchor points; a toggle for the ghost on the previous or the next frame. Agreed order: auto anchors + merge first, then the ghost.
- Model: `VectorPoint.auto` (the anchor was put there automatically). Saved without a format bump: the sparse anchor list of the Ink gets extra entries `AUTO_FLAG_OFFSET (1,000,000) + point index, 1`, which older builds skip (out of range for them); `toInk` / `toVectorStroke` read and write them. `VectorMerge.mid` and `VectorEraser.pointAt` carry the flag.
- New `AutoAnchors.kt`. `AutoAnchors.targets`: ends of an open line plus up to 6 tips (resample the control polygon, light smoothing, turning angle over a window of about 6 % of the length, local maxima above 100 - 70 x sensitivity degrees, minimum spacing 12 % of the length, tips near the ends dropped); a closed line gets its tips or two opposite points. `assignFresh` numbers them from the first number not in the taken set (marked auto). `refresh`: for every line with automatic anchors, clear them, find the new targets, reuse the number of the old automatic anchor nearest to a target (within 3 px or 3 % of the length), otherwise a free number; manual anchors stay and block their numbers; returns the same list object when nothing changed (so no empty undo step). `refreshFor(layer, cel, strokes, sens)` wraps it with the neighbour numbers.
- `AutoAnchors.inherit(stroke, ghost, blocked)`: the new line takes the ghost's anchor numbers, except numbers already used in its keyframe. Open lines: direction from the end distances (a line drawn the other way is read backwards), ghost ends land on the line's ends, other anchors at the nearest point of the new line with increasing position, falling back to the same relative arc length when the nearest point is far (more than 60 % of the larger bounding diagonal); closed lines: nearest point of each anchor, no order. A point is inserted where the line has no vertex within 0.4 % of its length. The inherited anchors are NOT automatic (they say which point matches which), so a later merge does not redo them.
- `LineGhost` (same file): `reference` (nearest previous / next keyframe with vector strokes whose drawing differs from the current one; clones and empty frames skipped), `unclaimed` (reference lines that no current line shares an anchor number with or lies on by its ends), `current(open, skip)`, `choose(stroke, open, shown, px)` (the shown line, unless the stroke lies clearly closer to another one; null when it lies on none, then it is just a new line).
- `Canvas.kt`: `vecAnchorNew(l, stroke)` runs when a freehand line (`vecCommitStroke`) or a Line / shape (`commitShape`) is committed: ghost on and a ghost line chosen -> inherit (the ghost line gets automatic anchors first through `replaceVectorCel`, a separate undo step, when it has none); otherwise `assignFresh` with the numbers used in this keyframe and in the neighbouring keyframes taken (so unrelated lines never pair by accident). After `VectorMerge.attach`, `vecRefreshAnchors` redoes the automatic anchors of the merged result. Ghost drawing: before the live stroke, the shown ghost line is drawn with `VectorTessellator` in cyan at 0.28-0.70 opacity (8 steps per 1.6 s cycle, so about 5 redraws a second) with its numbered anchor dots, the other untraced lines at 0.14; only on a visible, unlocked vector layer with the Brush (draw mode) or Line / shapes tool, not while playing.
- `VectorUi.kt`: draw / line bar gets Auto anchors (anchor icon), Ghost (sparkle), previous / next toggle and Skip (only while the ghost is on) and Tune (a panel with the tip sensitivity slider). The Delete and Merge points buttons redo the automatic anchors of the lines they changed.
- `Editor.kt`: `vecAutoAnchor` (default on), `vecTipSens` (0.5), `vecAnchorOpen`, `vecGhost` (default off), `vecGhostNext`, `vecGhostSkip`; session state, not saved.
- Files: `AutoAnchors.kt` (new), `Vector.kt`, `Editor.kt`, `Canvas.kt`, `VectorUi.kt`, `WhatsNew.kt` (note 53), `CHANGELOG.md`, `PROJECT_MEMORY.md`. Protected files untouched.
- Validation: source inspection and bracket balance only (Canvas.kt's paren count of -1 is the same in the original). NOT compiled, not run on a device. Untested: how well tips are found on real strokes (the thresholds are a first guess, expect to tune them); inheritance on closed lines and on lines drawn with fewer points than the ghost has anchors; the ghost on a keyframe that is a clone; undo of the extra "anchor the ghost" step; the shimmer cost on large drawings; old files (no anchors) opened with auto anchors on (only new lines get anchors; use Anchor on the old ones or trace them with the ghost on); auto anchors now exist on every new line, so selection mode shows numbered dots on all of them.

Name fields (cut, layer, folder names) still use the normal keyboard.
- (4) Cause: a Compose dialog is its own window and does not inherit the activity's hidden bars, so every AlertDialog showed them until it closed; text-field keyboards also bring them back without a focus change. `ImmersiveDialog.kt`: `HideSystemBarsInWindow()` (hides the bars in the dialog's window through `DialogWindowProvider`) and an `AlertDialog` with the Material 3 parameter list that calls it. It lives in the package, so files that star-import Material 3 pick it up with no change; the four files that imported the Material one explicitly (Canvas, Recovery, VectorUi, WhatsNew) lost that import (an explicit import would win). `MainActivity`: `onPostCreate` hides the bars at start; `guardBars()` hides them again when the keyboard finishes animating (`WindowInsetsAnimationCompat`); the existing `onWindowFocusChanged` re-hide stays. Dropdown menus and popups were not changed (the focus re-hide covers them); not verified on a device which buttons were the culprits.

## 2026-11-20 — Copy / Paste of the selection (drawing, background and vector layers)

- Lines: `copyLines` uses `VectorEdit.delete(strokes, allPoints - selected)`, which keeps whole selected lines and only the selected runs of partly selected ones. `pasteLines` appends them to the active layer's cel through `applyScope` (one undo step), keeps a line's id unless it clashes in the target cel (then `newStrokeId()`), bakes live fills (`fill` 2 -> 1) and selects the pasted points (`vecSelCels` / `vecSel`).

Paste is highlighted once something is copied. `ClipboardBar` (SelectionClipboard.kt, wired in `Canvas.kt`, bottom right): a Paste widget for drawing / background layers while the Selection tool is on and nothing is selected (the selection bar is not shown then).

## 2026-11-20 — Interpolation drag: only the selected lines, partners by anchor

- AI model: Claude
- Problem: with lines selected, other lines were re-interpolated too. Causes found in source: `interpolate` paired the leftover lines by geometry (`pairDifferent`) and faded every unpaired line of the first frame in or out; in three-frame mode the selection was resolved against the current playhead scope (`vecSelectedLineIds`, by id), so after moving the playhead (`sameCels` false) it came back empty and meant "all lines".
- `VectorInterpolation.pairSelected(a, b, chosen)`: only the chosen lines of the last frame `b` look for a partner in the first frame `a`: most shared anchor numbers (nearest centroid breaks ties), else the same id. No geometric fallback. `interpolateChosen(a, b, chosen?, mode, t, keepRest)`: chosen lines are blended with their partner, nothing else of `a` is used, nothing fades; `keepRest` returns `b` with those lines replaced in place (stacking order kept; this replaces the older fills-under / lines-over regrouping), otherwise only the blended lines (preview). A chosen line without a partner stays as in `b`. `interpolateSelected` (two frames) delegates to it; empty selection = everything, as before (`interpolate` unchanged).
- Three frames: `vecSelectedLinesIn(ed, b)` resolves the selection for the last selected frame from `ed.vecSelCels` (`VecScope(emptyList(), vecSelCels)`, so it survives playhead moves; same frame by index, other frames by line id). null = nothing selected (all lines); empty = selected but not found in the last frame (nothing interpolated, the bar says so). `interpolateIntoMiddle` / `interpolateIntoFrame` take these indices; middle-frame lines with the id of the partner or of the chosen line are replaced, others stay. Removed `interpolateIds`; `vecSelectedLineIds` is now unused.
- Files: `Vector.kt`, `Canvas.kt`, `VectorUi.kt`, `WhatsNew.kt` (note 49), `CHANGELOG.md`, `PROJECT_MEMORY.md`. Protected files untouched.
- Validation: source inspection and bracket balance only (Canvas.kt's paren count of -1 is the same in the original). NOT compiled, not run on a device. Untested: a selected line whose anchors exist only in the first frame, a selection made on the first frame (matched to the last by id only), refAll with several layers, stacking order of the created frame, the unmatched-selected-line case.

## 2026-11-19 (9) — Merge anywhere along lines, point-bar Merge button, interpolation drag on three frames

- AI model: Claude
- `VectorMerge.attach`: new crossing phase after the T-junction phase: where the body of the new line crosses the body of another line (centre-line segments, interior of both segments), both get a shared vertex (skipped when each already has a vertex within 0.6 px; max 12 per pair). Used by the Brush and Line / shapes tools with Merge on. Inserting a vertex on the chord of a spline line can shift the curve slightly there.
- `VectorMerge.mergePoints` replaces `joinSelected` (removed): the Merge button now lives in the point selection bar (Points on; the line-mode button is gone) and works on selected points: selected pairs merge at any distance (end+end join, same line's two ends close, end + inner point = the end moves onto that point, inner + inner meet at the midpoint); a point without a selected partner merges with the nearest other line within the Merge radius (end onto an end = join, else onto the body with an inserted vertex); a line with all points selected only offers its two ends and only within the radius. Plan-then-apply: moves, then insertions (per line, descending), then joins (the older line's identity and look win, `joinSharp` stamped), then closes. The Merge panel (Radius / Sharp joins) shows in that bar. `ed.vecDocZoom` is now refreshed on every composition (`vecZoom()` call in `Canvas.kt`); before, it was only written while drawing, so the old button could use a stale scale (my best guess for "doesn't work"; not verified).
- `VectorGaps`: a crossing with a vertex within 0.6 px on both lines, and an end sitting on a body vertex of the other line, are merged and no longer shimmer; ends meeting ends of separate lines still do.
- Interpolation drag, three frames: `vecSelectedFrames(layer, ed)` (ed.selCels on the layer, timeline order). Exactly 3: first and last are the references (`a.vecCfg` mode), `interpolateIntoFrame` replaces the middle frame with a fresh unique cel (no new frame; a clone of another frame is not changed along; `ed.selCels` / `vecSelCels` repointed, a selection on the old middle frame is cleared). Selected lines (`vecSelectedLineIds`, by stroke id) limit it to those lines; the other lines of the middle frame stay. Preview only while the playhead is on the middle frame. 2 or >=4 selected: the bar asks for three and the check does nothing; 0 or 1: the previous behaviour (including the selected-lines rule from (8)).

## 2026-11-19 (7) — Vector fill reaches small corners; CHANGELOG restored

- AI model: Claude
- Cause (from reading the code, not measured): a fill's outline was (1) smoothed with two [1 2 1]/4 passes on the traced ring, which pulls every sharp tip inward, and (2) drawn through `VectorTessellator.smoothThrough`, whose fillet rounds any turn between about 55 and 170 degrees with a radius up to 45% of the neighbouring segments, so narrow tips were cut off.
- Fix: `maskToFillPoints` no longer smooths the ring (RDP at 1 px takes the staircase out and keeps the tips); `fillPath` calls `smoothThrough(..., sharp = 1f)` (no fillet, bends over ~30 degrees stay corners; gentle curves are still smoothed). Existing fills are drawn with the sharp corners at once; live fills get the new outline on Refill.
- Not changed: the flood itself (gap closing, Grow, tolerance).
- Selection transform lag (reported together): NOT fixed. Nothing changed this session in the transform path (`VecLiveCel`, `vecThrottled`, `TileSet.liveVector`); the only touch is `vecZoom()` also writing the plain field `ed.vecDocZoom`. Possible costs found by reading, unmeasured: per-tile `BlurMaskFilter` / texture layer in `VectorTessellator.render` for soft, textured or aaSoft lines; large fills in the moving set. Waiting for which transform and what is selected.
- Process note: the CHANGELOG was truncated by my edit in (6) (file opened for writing before it was read) and rebuilt from the uploaded v47 file plus entries (4), (5), (6); the wording of (4) and (5) was re-created from my notes, not byte-identical.

## 2026-11-19 (3) — Line weight: irregular weight, anti-aliasing slider, light dial, live preview

Only when the previous keyframe is a single frame does the earlier rule apply (toggle on and current longer than one frame: take from the current; otherwise push later). Button tooltip updated.

- Tool: the Fill tool is allowed on vector layers (`Canvas.kt`); the existing raster flow (flood map, `smartFlood` with auto gap, Grow, drag for gap, preview) is reused. Only the pixel source (`vecFillPixels`: the layer's lines, or every visible drawing layer's lines / painted pixels with "Across layers", drawn black on white, fills excluded) and the commit differ: `maskToFillPoints` traces the region outline (`traceMaskOutline`, pixel-edge following), smooths the staircase and simplifies it (RDP 1 px), `addVectorFill` inserts the fill after the last older fill (so fills are under all lines; later fills above earlier ones) as one undo step, or recolours an existing fill of the same area (`VectorEdit.sameRegion`) when tapped inside it.
- Live fills: `refillLiveFills` / `resolveFillPoints` redo the flood from the stored seed / gap / grow / across against the keyframe's current lines (tick = the key's start frame), one undo step; buttons Refill (selected keyframes or current) and All frames in the new `VectorFillBar` (Live fill, Across layers). Not automatic after edits (undo bookkeeping of pen strokes depends on the stroke list), not recomputed for live-interpolated in-betweens (they morph like baked fills); Refill runs synchronously on the main thread.
- Transform lag, cause: every pen move cleared and re-encoded every stroke of the cel (`toInk`) and rebuilt every tile of the cel on the UI thread. Fix: `VectorEdit.move / elevate / transform` return the same stroke instance when nothing of it changes; new `VecLiveCel` (`Vector.kt`) keeps a base `TileSet` of the strokes that never move and per change resets only the tiles the moving strokes touch (old and new place) to the base and draws the moving strokes there (`TileSet.liveVector`, `TileSet.stampStroke`, no encode / decode); only changed strokes are re-encoded into `cel.strokes`; the set is pinned meanwhile; `finish` rebuilds the cel once only when a moved stroke was below a static one. `Canvas.kt`: `vecLiveCels`, `vecThrottled` (one applied edit per 14 ms for the point drag and the transform handles, the newest skipped one is applied on release), `vecFinishLive` at every drag end. Falls back to the old full path when the stroke count changes.
- Build fix (run of 2026-11-18, log `logs_120223789416`): the first build failed with "Unresolved reference" errors in `Canvas.kt`, `VectorUi.kt` and all of `VectorFill.kt` because the new file was declared `package com.exemple.inbetween` (the folder name) instead of `com.example.inbetween`. Corrected in `VectorFill.kt`. No other error was in the log.
- Files: `Vector.kt`, `Tiles.kt`, `Canvas.kt`, `Editor.kt`, `VectorUi.kt`, `VectorFill.kt` (new), `WhatsNew.kt`, `PROJECT_MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. Protected files untouched.
- Validation: source inspection, brace / paren balance. NOT compiled, not run, nothing measured. Untested: fill look and speed at full size, the outline trace on odd shapes and very large areas, islands (covered, not cut out), Refill on many keyframes, drag on a dense cel (z-order fix at release, point brushes touching new strokes rebuild the base), undo / redo after a drag and after a fill, fills and wobble / hardness together, exports.

## 2026-11-18 (7) — Brush look on vector lines: hardness, texture, tip shape / roundness / angle / follow

- AI model: Claude
- `VectorStroke` gains `hardness`, `texId`, `texAmt`, `texScale`, `texInvert`, `texImg`, `tipShape`, `roundness`, `tipAngle`, `tipFollow` (defaults = the plain line), `hasStyle()`, `tipActive()`, `withBrush(Brush)` (used by `vecCommitStroke`, `commitShape` and the live stroke in `Canvas.kt`) and `styleFrom` (the two interpolation constructors carry the look of stroke `a`; wobble is still not carried). Stored in the kind-5 Ink data text as extra `|` fields after the wobble ones (`id|brush|aa|wobble|scale|seed|hardness|texId|texAmt|texScale|texInvert|texImg|tipShape|roundness|tipAngle|tipFollow`), written in full only when `hasStyle()`; older strokes and older shorter texts read as the defaults. No format bump. A build before this change reading such a file would take the whole text after the first `|` as the brush id (unused for rendering).
- Tip shape: `VectorTessellator.tipFactor` scales each ribbon half width by the tip's extent across the line (ellipse for circle / etched, triangle, rectangle; axis = `tipAngle`, plus the line direction when `tipFollow`; triangle takes the mean of both sides). Caps stay round. Applied in `ribbon`, so path, strip, centerline-based hit areas that use `ribbon`, and bounds stay consistent.
- Edge softness and texture: new `VectorTessellator.render` (used by `TileSet.stampVector`, a software canvas): hardness < 1 sets a `BlurMaskFilter` of (1 - hardness) x the widest half width; a texture draws the line in a `saveLayer` and multiplies it with the brush's tile (`Textures.tile` / `BrushAssets.textureTile`, `texScale`, page-anchored) using DST_IN. `bounds` grows by 2.5 x that feather. The live preview (`draw`, hardware canvas) shows the tip shape but neither the blur nor the texture. Not used: Grain slider (`Brush.texture`), `texMoving`, Etched tip shape, dab spacing / jitter / wet / flow / capped.

## 2026-11-18 (5) — Rounded sharp turns, point-preserving vector brushes, selection widgets on vector layers

- AI model: Claude
- Report (screenshot): scribbled vector lines with sharp turns looked blocky / chamfered. Found in source: `smoothThrough` kept every turn over ~55 degrees as a hard corner, and `ribbon` bevelled the outer side of sharp joins, so a reversal ended in a flat cut.
- `Vector.kt` `VectorTessellator`: new `fillet` pass at the start of `smoothThrough` rounds each turn between ~55 and ~170 degrees with a circular arc (centreline radius max(1.2 px, 1.6 x half width), tangent distance at most 45% of the neighbouring segments, 18-degree steps). Only turns beyond ~170 degrees remain corners (`REVERSAL_COS`). `ribbon` now emits a round join (arc of up to 8 steps on the outer side) instead of a bevel; its output arrays grow on demand. `centerline` / `VectorSimplify.fit` see the same curve. Side effect: shape-tool corners (rectangles) are rounded slightly. New `VectorTessellator.curveAt` (point on the drawn curve).
- `VectorBrush` (Shift / Smoothen / Distort): `densify` (which added points along every segment near the brush) is replaced by `ensureHandle`: existing points are moved; one point is inserted, on the drawn curve nearest to the brush centre, only when no control point lies inside the radius. `apply` signature unchanged.
- `VectorUi.kt`: with the Selection tool, `VectorWidgetBar` gets a top row with the regular selection widgets: Add, Erase (`ed.selOp`, used by `selOpOf`), Invert (all points minus the selection), aspect lock (`ed.xfLock`), rotation magnet (`MagnetBtn`) and Done (clears `vecSel`). `Canvas.kt` `vecXfMove` now applies `ed.rotationSnap` (absolute 30 degrees) to vector selection rotation. Fill and Transform from the raster `SelectionBar` are not shown: the vector handles are always active.
- Files: `Vector.kt`, `VectorUi.kt`, `Canvas.kt`, `WhatsNew.kt`, `PROJECT_MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. No format change. Protected files untouched.
- Validation: source inspection, brace / paren balance. NOT compiled, not run on a device, and the new look was not rendered. Untested: how round the scribble turns look at your line widths (radius constants may need tuning), cost of the extra points on dense scribbles, closed shapes, round-join winding in the even-odd ring, the brushes on sparse lines, Invert, rotation magnet.
